# STEA-004 Autonomous Plane-Flight Execution Plan
## Continue ACMS + Server Manager + Agent Runtime Manager + PDU + Hermes Work After REV4 Handoff

**Date:** 2026-09-27  
**Execution window:** Long autonomous session while human is unavailable (~10-hour flight)  
**Primary agent:** STEA-004 Entrepreneur

## Authority

Continue as far as safely possible inside the already approved architecture.

You are authorized to:

- implement;
- test;
- create PRs;
- self-review;
- merge when repository/release gates pass;
- deploy to staging/production when the existing safe-release gates pass;
- create TDRs and Future Work;
- make ordinary reversible implementation decisions;
- continue from one completed phase to the next without waiting for the human.

Stop only when a true human/product decision is required or a safety/rollback boundary is crossed.

---

# 1. Mission

Do **not** redo completed REV4 work.

Continue by closing the remaining operational gaps and then implementing the newly approved ACMS memory/context-cost architecture.

Highest-value order:

```text
1. Hermes state sync on acms-worker-001
2. ARM desired-state reconciler
3. ACMS runtime lifecycle integration
4. PDU consumer integration
5. full LLM Manager SQLAlchemy/Alembic migration
6. legacy AgentManager decommission/parity
7. ACMS memory/session requirements
8. ACMS Memory/Session Offload MVP
9. per-Work cost/budget controls
10. SSE + Attention/Audit if time remains
```

---

Treat the latest HANDOFF as authoritative unless live verification shows a newer compatible state.


Do not destroy or repurpose them.

---

# 3. Remaining work from the handoff

Explicitly unfinished:

- ARM desired-state reconciler;
- disposable destroy/recreate validation;
- full LLM Manager ORM migration;
- PDU consumer/service-identity completion;
- per-agent Hermes state sync on VM124;
- ARM/PDU lifecycle correlation;
- legacy AgentManager decommission.

Newly approved architecture from the AI–Human SOP:

- Work may span many disposable execution sessions;
- ACMS stores structured operational memory;
- fresh sessions receive compact selected context;
- handoffs make session rotation safe;
- track current context separately from cumulative API tokens/spend;
- support economic checkpoint/rotation thresholds;
- support per-Work soft/hard cloud budgets;
- send compact semantic summaries upward;
- Executive Agents should eventually reconstruct from ACMS instead of keeping giant perpetual sessions.

---

# 4. Execution-context discipline

This sprint should model the intended workflow.

```text
Human + ChatGPT = WHY
ACMS            = WHAT / WHO / STATUS / COST / RESULT
Git + Markdown  = WHAT CHANGED
Hermes          = HOW for the current bounded task
```

At each PR-sized phase:

```text
finish atomic work
→ tests
→ commit/push
→ concise phase handoff/checkpoint
→ start next phase from compact state
```

Do not keep reloading full historical logs/transcripts.

Cloud context guidance:

```text
<100k tokens       continue
100k–150k          prepare checkpoint
>150k              strongly prefer handoff + fresh context
```

Checkpoint earlier if cumulative API tokens/spend are growing disproportionately.

If `/new` cannot be initiated autonomously, still write a compact phase handoff and avoid pulling old context forward.

---

# 5. Phase 0 — baseline verification

Verify only what is necessary:

- current main SHAs;
- VM114 health/version;
- Server Manager API;
- ARM DB revision;
- ACMS CT122 health/version;
- PDU VM156 health/version;
- VM124 bridge health;
- VM906 untouched.

Do not rerun destructive E2E merely to prove already documented facts.

Create a concise baseline checkpoint.

# 6. Phase 1 — finish Hermes state backup on VM124

## Goal

Move `state_sync` from PENDING to verified/healthy.

VM124 should have:

```text
dedicated hermes-base-setup branch
automatic periodic sync
secret scan
successful safe commit/push
observable sync health
```

## Work

1. inspect actual Hermes/profile/state paths on VM124;
2. install agent-specific sync using the existing `hermes-base-setup` mechanism;
3. do not copy VM906 identity/secrets;
4. use existing authorized Git automation credentials from external secret storage;
5. install cron or systemd timer;
6. make one harmless Markdown state change;
7. verify secret scan;
8. commit/push to the dedicated worker branch;
9. record latest state SHA/time/health in ARM if supported;
10. verify persistence across VM restart.

If feasible, add an immediate task-completion state-sync hook. If that creates large coupling, document it and implement a simpler explicit hook first.

Acceptance:

- periodic sync installed;
- manual sync green;
- remote commit visible;
- secret probe rejected;
- no commits to main/master;
- reboot preserves sync;
- backup health is observable or a concrete follow-up is recorded.

Checkpoint.

---

# 7. Phase 2 — implement ARM desired-state reconciler

The API currently records desired state; make it execute safely.

Required behavior:

```text
desired RUNNING
→ observe
→ start owned VM if stopped
→ wait for OS/bridge readiness
→ update actual state

desired STOPPED
→ preserve/checkpoint state as policy requires
→ graceful shutdown
→ verify stopped
→ DO NOT destroy persistent VM

recovery
→ bounded retries/backoff
→ preserve ACMS identity
```

Safety:

- act only on ARM-owned VMs with verified ownership marker;
- ownership mismatch fails closed;
- never touch VM906;
- no Kubernetes work.

Implement:

- reconcile loop;
- lock/single-replica safety;
- retry/backoff;
- `last_reconcile_at`;
- `last_error`;
- restart/recovery count;
- audit;
- desired vs actual state.

Tests:

- no-op when already correct;
- RUNNING starts once;
- STOPPED shuts down once;
- ownership mismatch;
- PVE unavailable;
- slow bridge recovery;
- reconciler restart/idempotency.

Deploy and verify VM124 through one safe API-driven stop/start cycle.

Checkpoint.

# 8. Phase 3 — ACMS runtime lifecycle integration

Update ACMS to consume real ARM lifecycle state.

Implement:

- desired runtime state;
- actual runtime state;
- recovery/error state;
- Administrator:
  - Start Runtime
  - Stop Runtime
  - Restart Runtime
  - Reconcile Now
- runtime events in semantic event/audit history.

Keep runtime controls distinct from:

```text
A2A Interrupt
A2A Cancel
Steer
task/session controls
```

If active Work remains assigned while runtime is unavailable, preserve Work state and surface the external runtime dependency.

Do not add casual persistent-worker deletion.

Checkpoint.

# 9. Phase 4 — integrate PDU consumers

PDU Manager API hardening is already live.

## Server Manager / ARM

Use the common PDU client for asset-based operations.

Implement high-level functions such as:

```text
get_power_state(asset_id)
plan_power_action(...)
ensure_powered_on(...)
request_reboot(...)
get_power_job(...)
```

Coordinate Proxmox/OS/runtime state around physical power.

Remember:

```text
PDU ON != server ready
```

## ACMS

Implement direct ACMS support for:

- PDU health;
- capabilities;
- asset power state;
- dry-run/action planning;
- job lookup;
- audit/correlation lookup.

Create or reuse an `InfrastructureOperation` durable record.

Correlation should allow:

```text
ACMS Work/Agent
→ infrastructure operation
→ Server Manager operation if applicable
→ PDU job
→ PDU audit
```

Write-capable ACMS methods may be implemented behind Administrator/service authorization.

**Do not perform a real physical ON/OFF/REBOOT during the flight unless the current execution context contains explicit authorization for the exact asset/action.**

Read-only and dry-run validation are allowed.

## LLM Manager

Prefer:

```text
LLM Manager → Server Manager → PDU
```

for compute-node lifecycle.

Checkpoint.

# 10. Phase 5 — continue full LLM Manager SQLAlchemy/Alembic migration

Human direction: full migration remains the target.

First produce a table inventory:

```text
table
owner
authoritative?
row count
read paths
write paths
target SQLAlchemy model
migration status
risk
```

Migrate in domain-sized slices:

1. model registry/logical routes;
2. deployment/revision history;
3. benchmark/qualification;
4. LLM-owned usage/cost metadata;
5. remaining LLM Manager-owned configuration/history;
6. remove legacy psycopg2 paths only after parity.

Do not copy LiteLLM-owned tables just to claim completion; use a defined adapter if LiteLLM remains authoritative.

Rules:

- SQLAlchemy 2.x;
- Alembic for owned schema;
- async where appropriate;
- explicit SQL/Core allowed for complex operations;
- preserve IDs/history;
- validate row counts/content;
- stage before prod;
- backup/rollback before destructive migration;
- no ACMS DB coupling.

Checkpoint after each domain.

# 12. Phase 7 — codify ACMS memory/context/cost requirements

Update ACMS `REQUIREMENTS.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, sprint planning, and ADR/TDRs.

Use next available IDs after ACMS-REQ-054.

Recommended approved requirements:

### ACMS-REQ-055 — Work continuity across disposable execution sessions
A Work Item/Program survives across many execution sessions.

### ACMS-REQ-056 — Explicit execution Context Packages
Record the selected context supplied to each session.

### ACMS-REQ-057 — Handoff completeness before rotation
Verify Work ID, status, Git, tests, deployment, decisions, debt, remaining work, next action, artifacts.

### ACMS-REQ-058 — Economic execution-session telemetry
Track separately:
- current context;
- max context;
- utilization;
- cumulative API tokens;
- estimated spend;
- session age;
- context growth rate.

### ACMS-REQ-059 — Work/session cloud budgets
Soft threshold = checkpoint/review/reroute.  
Hard threshold = no new cloud execution without override.

### ACMS-REQ-060 — Relevant-context selection
Select relevant hierarchical context; do not inject everything.

### ACMS-REQ-061 — Compact upward reporting
Prefer semantic summaries + artifact references over full logs/diffs.

### ACMS-REQ-062 — Disposable Executive Agent sessions
Persistent executive identity, fresh reasoning sessions reconstructed from ACMS/handoffs.

Architecture statement:

```text
ChatGPT remembers WHY
ACMS remembers WHAT / WHO / STATUS / COST / RESULT
Git remembers WHAT CHANGED
Hermes temporarily remembers HOW
```

Do not claim ACMS replaces Human+ChatGPT durable design memory.

Commit the documentation slice before implementation.

Checkpoint.


```text
→ Session 1 + Context Package
→ handoff/checkpoint

```

# 14. Phase 9 — Work budgets and cost controls

If time remains:

Use authoritative Server/LLM Manager usage metrics.

Add per-Work visibility for:

- cumulative cloud tokens;
- estimated spend;
- model/provider;
- session breakdown;
- soft budget;
- hard budget.

Soft threshold:

- semantic event;
- checkpoint recommendation;
- Attention/advisory;
- consider local reroute.

Hard threshold:

- block **new** cloud execution;
- do not interrupt unsafe atomic work;
- require authorized override.

Never fabricate costs when source data is unavailable.

---

# 15. Phase 10 — SSE + Attention/Audit if time remains

Continue the previously approved ACMS roadmap:

```text
browser SSE semantic-event stream
→ Attention/Audit UI
```

Useful Attention triggers now include:

- UNREACHABLE;
- runtime recovery exhausted;
- critical context/cost threshold;
- failed/rejected execution;
- human-input handoff;
- mandatory approval;
- Hermes state-backup failure.

Ordinary work/session mismatch remains audit + automatic reconciliation first; escalate only if persistent/scope-significant.

---

# 16. Do not guess these product areas while the human is answering architecture questions

Do not implement large speculative behavior for:

- External Agent Gateway customer policy;
- `agentify.me.co` portal product behavior;
- detailed Speed-to-Lead domain/configuration model;
- billing model;
- customer data-retention policy;
- Kubernetes production runtime.

You may:

- update Future Work;
- add Problems to Solve;
- draft Proposed ADRs;
- document interface boundaries already implied by accepted architecture.

Do not mark unresolved product choices Accepted.

---

# 17. Safety and destructive boundaries

Persistent worker policy:

```text
STOP when idle
do not destroy by default
```

Destruction only for:

- explicitly disposable test runtime;
- explicit retirement;
- verified corrupt replacement;
- proven ARM ownership;
- durable state already preserved.

No real PDU hardware actuation without exact asset/action authorization.

No secret values in commits, logs, handoffs, or chat.

---

# 18. TDR discipline

Update/retire:

- LLM Manager ORM migration debt;
- service token → mTLS;
- Git/cron agent-state sync → future Agent State service;
- single-replica reconciler leadership;
- qga long-operation risk;
- legacy AgentManager overlap;
- staging DB parity.

Any new shortcut needs:

```text
why accepted
risk
owner
repayment trigger
```

# 20. Definition of success

A successful flight does not require every phase to finish.

It requires:

- no regression of the proven E2E worker chain;
- meaningful deferred work closed;
- independently verified slices shipped;
- compact checkpoints between phases;
- no protected-resource incident;
- no secret leakage;
- no speculative customer architecture silently accepted;
- exact remaining work documented.

Continue down the priority list until the execution window ends or a real stop condition is reached.

Never put secrets in release metadata.\n- Feature-specific smoke hooks via `ACMS_FEATURE_SMOKE` env (plan §11).\n\n## Live CT122 quirks (validated 2026-09-26)\n\n- Git checkout is at `/opt/acms/repo` (NOT `/opt/acms`); `.env` at\n  `/opt/acms/.env` (0600, key names only — never echo values).\n- **Compose v2 container names are `acms-<service>-1`** — bare\n  `docker exec acms-app` FAILS (\"No such container\"). If a manual hop stage dies on a bare\n  name AFTER copying the maintenance conf, the host\n  `deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` is already switched —\n  `git checkout -- deploy/reverse-proxy/nginx.conf` BEFORE retrying, or the\n  retry double-applies maintenance content.\n- No `pg_dump`/`psql` on the CT host — run inside the postgres container:\n  `docker compose -f deploy/compose.yaml --env-file /opt/acms/.env exec -T\n  postgres pg_dump -U acms -Fc acms`.\n- No `curl` in the app image — probe with `docker exec acms-acms-app-1\n  python -c \"import urllib.request; ...\"` on 127.0.0.1:8000.\n- nginx allowlists 10.0.10.0/24 + 10.0.20.0/24 — validate via the VM's own IP\n  `https://10.0.20.122` with `curl -k` (self-signed; MARION has NO home.arpa\n  DNS — raw IPs everywhere).\n- `/version` returns `{version, git_sha, git_sha_short, build_time}` — the\n  git_sha identity check catches wrong-image states instantly. Run it after\n  ANY container recreation.\n- LLDAP 10.0.20.101:3890, base dc=example,dc=com; groups acms-admin/16\n  (jordatech), acms-workers/17, acms-observers/18.\n\n## Docker/Compose pitfalls (from the live drill — see references file)\n\n- **Single-file bind mounts pin the inode**: swapping the file on disk is\n  invisible to the container; HUP reload then serves stale config forever.\n  nginx now uses a directory mount (`./reverse-proxy:/etc/nginx/conf.d`) and\n  the maintenance template is named `*.conf.template` so the include glob\n  never loads it. Never re-introduce a single-file conf mount.\n- **`docker compose up -d <svc>` recreates depends_on services too** and can\n  bring the app back with the default image (`acms-app:local`). Pin\n  `ACMS_APP_IMAGE_TAG=<tag>` on EVERY compose up in tooling.\n- **Never command-substitute `compose build`** — its stdout is the build log\n  and pollutes captured variables (`invalid tag`). Send build output to\n  `>&2`; compute the tag, never capture it.\n- **pipefail + `grep -c` false positive**: `grep -c` exits 1 when the count\n  is 0; capture the count into a variable with `|| true`, compare numerically.\n- Recovery from stuck maintenance mode: `up -d --force-recreate\n  reverse-proxy`, then re-pin the app image, then verify via `/version`.\n\n## Bootstrap hop (chicken-and-egg first deploy)\n\nThe first deployment of the release tooling cannot use the tooling. Manual\nhop with identical guarantees: verified backup → stop app → checkout target\nSHA → build SHA-tagged image → migrate → start → validate → write release\nmetadata (mark `mode=manual-bootstrap`). Proven 2026-09-26 (~2 min window).\n\n## Verification hygiene (file-tool corruption lesson)\n\nGround truth for any file change is `git diff` / `py_compile` / `bash -n` —\nnever the tool result echo. Echoes can mislead in BOTH directions (phantom\nerrors and phantom success; this session had mid-write corruption injecting\ngarbage text). For large/critical writes: verify immediately after writing,\nand if corrupted, repair via a python rewrite with assertion pre-checks on\nknown anchor lines, then re-verify with git diff.\n\n## Pointers\n\n- `references/live-drill-2026-09-26.md` — bug-by-bug drill breakdown, exact\n  commands, recovery timeline.\n- `references/hermes-harness-live-tests-2026-09.md` — Hermes 0.17.0 live\n  control-mapping results (pause/resume unsupported, steer/interrupt verified),\n  scratch-profile setup recipe, bridge-to-ACMS wiring.\n- `references/hermes-worker-bringup.md` — the full worker-runtime bring-up recipe\n  (VM clone → Hermes venv install via qga → LLM gateway wiring incl. context caps →\n  api-server platform config shape → systemd unit → verification + traps) as\n  executed on acms-worker-001.\n- Repo docs: `deploy/README.md` (operator runbook), ADR-0009 + ADR-0010 (both\n  Accepted 2026-09-26/27), `docs/HARNESS_CONTROL_MAPPING.md`, `SPRINT.md`\n  (current slices), handoffs under `docs/handoffs/`.\n\n## Combined slice 3+4 state (deployed 2026-09-27, v0.6.0 @ 85f3c92)\n\n- **ADR-0009 Accepted** (amended: live evidence + explicit deployment-authority\n  policy); **ADR-0010 Accepted** — heartbeat 60s / STALE 300s / reconcile\n  3600s / fleet 86400s / alignment grace 120s / context warnings 70-85-95,\n  all config-backed via `ACMS_*` settings (settings.py gained the fields).\n- Human-readable keys `ACMS-WORK-000001` / `ACMS-ASG-000001` via the\n  `acms_key_counters` transactional counter table — never row counts, never\n  reused; allocated in `create_work_item` / `assign_primary`.\n- State dimensions stay separate (plan §5): connectivity is ACMS-derived\n  (UNKNOWN/HEALTHY/STALE/UNREACHABLE from contact recency), `agent_running`\n  is harness-reported tri-state — the UI shows both as independent facts.\n- Heartbeat = complete snapshot (`acms-heartbeat-v1`), unknown fields rejected\n  by the Pydantic model; semantic events only on material change (never\n  per-heartbeat spam); event sequences allocated via the same counter table.\n- Time-dependent tests: inject `now_fn` into TelemetryScheduler; watch for\n  tz-naive vs tz-aware datetimes when mixing `datetime.now(timezone.utc)` with\n  DB-loaded values (replace(tzinfo=utc) guard in the stale delta).\n- **Next steps logged in agents.md:** wire `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON` into\n  CT122 env + dispatch/control buttons in the UI; slices 5–9 per master plan.\n\n## JINT-001 — Server Manager integration (live 2026-09-27, prod e1cd84ed, alembic 0005)\n\n- `acms/server_manager_client.py` (urllib, contract pinned Server Manager API 1.0.0) + `/api/v1/server-manager` routes: POST /agents (persistent identity reservation + `provisioning_requests` row with authority provenance per REV4 §14: human_in_acms | human_in_server_manager | pre-authorized_sprint_execution_context), POST /requests/{id}/provision (calls SM; ownership refusals → 403, SM errors → 502), GET /requests/{id} (correlates live job/runtime state). Migrations `0004_provisioning_requests`, `0005_prov_attempt`.\n- **State honesty (live-found bug):** the provision route MUST reflect the returned SM job state — SM job FAILED → request FAILED with the error, DONE → LIVE + runtime_id correlation via `list_runtimes`, else PROVISIONING. Unconditionally writing PROVISIONING stuck a failed request permanently (blocking both retry and truth). ACMS prod now runs this fix.\n- **Retry semantics (live-found):** same request_id replays the original (failed) SM job forever; deriving `|retry:N` by counting markers recomputes N=1 forever. Real fix = monotonic `attempt` column (migration 0005, default 1, increment per retry → `request_id|retry:N`).\n- **create_runtime timeout:** provisioning is synchronous server-side (clone+boot = minutes); the 30 s client default timed out while the job completed — ACMS then recorded FAILED for a LIVE runtime. `create_runtime` now uses timeout=600; other calls stay at the default.\n- **Honest-state correctness chained into worker bootstrap:** the request that hit the timeout recorded FAILED while VM 124 was actually LIVE — corrected manually via SQL (state='LIVE', runtime_id, job_id). When reconciling such rows, pull truth from the SM side (`GET /api/v1/agent-runtimes?acms_agent_id=`), never assume.\n- **Pitfalls found live:**\n  - **compose `environment:` enumerates explicitly** — new ACMS_* settings NEVER reach the container from `--env-file` alone; add passthrough lines to `deploy/compose.yaml` (live symptom: \"Server Manager integration not configured\" 503 despite env in /opt/acms/.env). Applied to `ACMS_SERVER_MANAGER_*` AND `ACMS_BRIDGE_TARGETS_JSON` — grep compose when adding any setting.\n  - **`env_prefix=\"ACMS_\"`** — Settings fields map to `ACMS_<FIELD>` env vars; naming them `SERVER_MANAGER_*` (no prefix) silently leaves settings empty. Check the prefix before writing env lines.\n  - **CT122 nginx 403s 127.0.0.1-originated API calls from inside the CT** (allowlist covers the CT IP, not loopback) — call ACMS APIs via `https://10.0.20.122`, not `https://127.0.0.1`.\n  - **ACMS repo has GitHub issues DISABLED** — reference `ACMS-REQ-###` in commit messages instead of creating issues (issue creation fails with \"has disabled issues\").\n  - **Bridge chat shape:** `/api/sessions/{sid}/chat` expects `{\"message\": str}`; the `{\"messages\": [...]}` array shape 400s with `missing_message` (steer + send_work session-bound path both fixed, PRs #20/#21).\n\n## Worker runtime facts (acms-worker-001, provisioned via the full chain 2026-09-27)\n\n- VM 124 on miam00111 @ 10.0.20.203 (DHCP), runtime_id 56c1b849-5407-452b-b326-b6e3ff7000e1, ACMS agent 22ac1b19-b7c9-4fd8-bf70-0b8e654ee026, cloned from template VM 121 (VM103-lineage Ubuntu 24.04 + legacy st-agentd; hostname carries `miam00111-vllm-vm103` lineage — cosmetic).\n- §8 ownership marker lives in the VM's PVE `description` field (full JSON: server_runtime_id / acms_agent_id / request_id / created_by_agent_runtime_manager=true / template_source) — written at provision time by ARM; the destroy path refuses without it.\n- Worker Hermes: venv `/opt/hermes-venv` (hermes-agent via pip; aiohttp REQUIRED for the api-server platform), profile `acms-worker-001`, bridge = `hermes-bridge.service` running `hermes gateway run --profile acms-worker-001 --replace` (gateway run takes NO --host/--port — they go in the profile config under `platforms.api_server.extra.{host,port,key}`; top-level host/port keys are IGNORED, defaults to loopback:8642). `API_SERVER_KEY` also required as env (both extra.key and the env var).\n- Worker LLM path: profile provider `llm-manager` → `http://10.0.20.108:8080/v1` (nginx plain-LAN surface on VM114 — self-signed TLS on :443 breaks OpenAI clients; bearer key is the auth layer on the private network). The VM114 sites-enabled file has DIVERGED from sites-available (enabled = live v2 TLS config).\n- Worker bootstrap runs via node qga (`qm guest exec 124 -- bash -c ...`): stage scripts as base64 files, run under nohup for long installs (qga channel wedges on long execs).", "path": "entrepreneurship/acms-project-operations/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/entrepreneurship/acms-project-operations", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/hermes-worker-bringup.md", "references/live-drill-2026-09-26.md", "references/hermes-harness-live-tests-2026-09.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["ACMS", "deployment", "rollback", "docker-compose", "jordan-workflow"]}}}

{"success": true, "name": "github-pr-workflow", "description": "GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.", "tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"], "content": "---\nname: github-pr-workflow\ndescription: \"GitHub PR lifecycle: branch, commit, open, CI, merge.\"\nversion: 1.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [GitHub, Pull-Requests, CI/CD, Git, Automation, Merge]\n    related_skills: [github-auth, github-code-review]\n---\n\n# GitHub Pull Request Workflow\n\nComplete guide for managing the PR lifecycle. Each section shows the `gh` way first, then the `git` + `curl` fallback for machines without `gh`.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Authenticated with GitHub (see `github-auth` skill)\n- Inside a git repository with a GitHub remote\n\n### Quick Auth Detection\n\n```bash\n# Determine which method to use throughout this workflow\nif command -v gh &>/dev/null && gh auth status &>/dev/null; then\n  AUTH=\"gh\"\nelse\n  AUTH=\"git\"\n  # Ensure we have a token for API calls\n  if [ -z \"$GITHUB_TOKEN\" ]; then\n    if _hermes_env=\"${HERMES_HOME:-$HOME/.hermes}/.env\"; [ -f \"$_hermes_env\" ] && grep -q \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\"; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"^GITHUB_TOKEN=\" \"$_hermes_env\" | head -1 | cut -d= -f2 | tr -d '\\n\\r')\n    elif grep -q \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null; then\n      GITHUB_TOKEN=$(grep \"github.com\" ~/.git-credentials 2>/dev/null | head -1 | sed 's|https://[^:]*:\\([^@]*\\)@.*|\\1|')\n    fi\n  fi\nfi\necho \"Using: $AUTH\"\n```\n\n### Extracting Owner/Repo from the Git Remote\n\nMany `curl` commands need `owner/repo`. Extract it from the git remote:\n\n```bash\n# Works for both HTTPS and SSH remote URLs\nREMOTE_URL=$(git remote get-url origin)\nOWNER_REPO=$(echo \"$REMOTE_URL\" | sed -E 's|.*github\\.com[:/]||; s|\\.git$||')\nOWNER=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f1)\nREPO=$(echo \"$OWNER_REPO\" | cut -d/ -f2)\necho \"Owner: $OWNER, Repo: $REPO\"\n```\n\n---\n\n## 1. Branch Creation\n\nThis part is pure `git` — identical either way:\n\n```bash\n# Make sure you're up to date\ngit fetch origin\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# Create and switch to a new branch\ngit checkout -b feat/add-user-authentication\n```\n\nBranch naming conventions:\n- `feat/description` — new features\n- `fix/description` — bug fixes\n- `refactor/description` — code restructuring\n- `docs/description` — documentation\n- `ci/description` — CI/CD changes\n\n## 2. Making Commits\n\nUse the agent's file tools (`write_file`, `patch`) to make changes, then commit:\n\n```bash\n# Stage specific files\ngit add src/auth.py src/models/user.py tests/test_auth.py\n\n# Commit with a conventional commit message\ngit commit -m \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\n\n- Add login/register endpoints\n- Add User model with password hashing\n- Add auth middleware for protected routes\n- Add unit tests for auth flow\"\n```\n\nCommit message format (Conventional Commits):\n```\ntype(scope): short description\n\nLonger explanation if needed. Wrap at 72 characters.\n```\n\nTypes: `feat`, `fix`, `refactor`, `docs`, `test`, `ci`, `chore`, `perf`\n\n## 3. Pushing and Creating a PR\n\n### Push the Branch (same either way)\n\n```bash\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n```\n\n### Create the PR\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\ngh pr create \\\n  --title \"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\" \\\n  --body \"## Summary\n- Adds login and register API endpoints\n- JWT token generation and validation\n\n## Test Plan\n- [ ] Unit tests pass\n\nCloses #42\"\n```\n\nOptions: `--draft`, `--reviewer user1,user2`, `--label \"enhancement\"`, `--base develop`\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.v3+json\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"title\\\": \\\"feat: add JWT-based user authentication\\\",\n    \\\"body\\\": \\\"## Summary\\nAdds login and register API endpoints.\\n\\nCloses #42\\\",\n    \\\"head\\\": \\\"$BRANCH\\\",\n    \\\"base\\\": \\\"main\\\"\n  }\"\n```\n\nThe response JSON includes the PR `number` — save it for later commands.\n\nTo create as a draft, add `\"draft\": true` to the JSON body.\n\n## 4. Monitoring CI Status\n\n### Check CI Status\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# One-shot check\ngh pr checks\n\n# Watch until all checks finish (polls every 10s)\ngh pr checks --watch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\n# Get the latest commit SHA on the current branch\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n# Query the combined status\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nprint(f\\\"Overall: {data['state']}\\\")\nfor s in data.get('statuses', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {s['context']}: {s['state']} - {s.get('description', '')}\\\")\"\n\n# Also check GitHub Actions check runs (separate endpoint)\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/check-runs \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\ndata = json.load(sys.stdin)\nfor cr in data.get('check_runs', []):\n    print(f\\\"  {cr['name']}: {cr['status']} / {cr['conclusion'] or 'pending'}\\\")\"\n```\n\n### Poll Until Complete (git + curl)\n\n```bash\n# Simple polling loop — check every 30 seconds, up to 10 minutes\nSHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\nfor i in $(seq 1 20); do\n  STATUS=$(curl -s \\\n    -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n    https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/commits/$SHA/status \\\n    | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['state'])\")\n  echo \"Check $i: $STATUS\"\n  if [ \"$STATUS\" = \"success\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"failure\" ] || [ \"$STATUS\" = \"error\" ]; then\n    break\n  fi\n  sleep 30\ndone\n```\n\n## 5. Auto-Fixing CI Failures\n\nWhen CI fails, diagnose and fix. This loop works with either auth method.\n\n### Step 1: Get Failure Details\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# List recent workflow runs on this branch\ngh run list --branch $(git branch --show-current) --limit 5\n\n# View failed logs\ngh run view <RUN_ID> --log-failed\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\n\n# List workflow runs on this branch\ncurl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs?branch=$BRANCH&per_page=5\" \\\n  | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, json\nruns = json.load(sys.stdin)['workflow_runs']\nfor r in runs:\n    print(f\\\"Run {r['id']}: {r['name']} - {r['conclusion'] or r['status']}\\\")\"\n\n# Get failed job logs (download as zip, extract, read)\nRUN_ID=<run_id>\ncurl -s -L \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/actions/runs/$RUN_ID/logs \\\n  -o /tmp/ci-logs.zip\ncd /tmp && unzip -o ci-logs.zip -d ci-logs && cat ci-logs/*.txt\n```\n\n### Step 2: Fix and Push\n\nAfter identifying the issue, use file tools (`patch`, `write_file`) to fix it:\n\n```bash\ngit add <fixed_files>\ngit commit -m \"fix: resolve CI failure in <check_name>\"\ngit push\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify\n\nRe-check CI status using the commands from Section 4 above.\n\n### Auto-Fix Loop Pattern\n\nWhen asked to auto-fix CI, follow this loop:\n\n1. Check CI status → identify failures\n2. Read failure logs → understand the error\n3. Use `read_file` + `patch`/`write_file` → fix the code\n4. `git add . && git commit -m \"fix: ...\" && git push`\n5. Wait for CI → re-check status\n6. Repeat if still failing (up to 3 attempts, then ask the user)\n\n## 6. Merging\n\n**With gh:**\n\n```bash\n# Squash merge + delete branch (cleanest for feature branches)\ngh pr merge --squash --delete-branch\n\n# Enable auto-merge (merges when all checks pass)\ngh pr merge --auto --squash --delete-branch\n```\n\n**With git + curl:**\n\n```bash\nPR_NUMBER=<number>\n\n# Merge the PR via API (squash)\ncurl -s -X PUT \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER/merge \\\n  -d \"{\n    \\\"merge_method\\\": \\\"squash\\\",\n    \\\"commit_title\\\": \\\"feat: add user authentication (#$PR_NUMBER)\\\"\n  }\"\n\n# Delete the remote branch after merge\nBRANCH=$(git branch --show-current)\ngit push origin --delete $BRANCH\n\n# Switch back to main locally\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\ngit branch -d $BRANCH\n```\n\nMerge methods: `\"merge\"` (merge commit), `\"squash\"`, `\"rebase\"`\n\n### Enable Auto-Merge (curl)\n\n```bash\n# Auto-merge requires the repo to have it enabled in settings.\n# This uses the GraphQL API since REST doesn't support auto-merge.\nPR_NODE_ID=$(curl -s \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls/$PR_NUMBER \\\n  | python3 -c \"import sys,json; print(json.load(sys.stdin)['node_id'])\")\n\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \\\n  https://api.github.com/graphql \\\n  -d \"{\\\"query\\\": \\\"mutation { enablePullRequestAutoMerge(input: {pullRequestId: \\\\\\\"$PR_NODE_ID\\\\\\\", mergeMethod: SQUASH}) { clientMutationId } }\\\"}\"\n```\n\n## 7. Complete Workflow Example\n\n```bash\n# 1. Start from clean main\ngit checkout main && git pull origin main\n\n# 2. Branch\ngit checkout -b fix/login-redirect-bug\n\n# 3. (Agent makes code changes with file tools)\n\n# 4. Commit\ngit add src/auth/login.py tests/test_login.py\ngit commit -m \"fix: correct redirect URL after login\n\nPreserves the ?next= parameter instead of always redirecting to /dashboard.\"\n\n# 5. Push\ngit push -u origin HEAD\n\n# 6. Create PR (picks gh or curl based on what's available)\n# ... (see Section 3)\n\n# 7. Monitor CI (see Section 4)\n\n# 8. Merge when green (see Section 6)\n```\n\n## Useful PR Commands Reference\n\n| Action | gh | git + curl |\n|--------|-----|-----------|\n| List my PRs | `gh pr list --author @me` | `curl -s -H \"Authorization: token $GITHUB_TOKEN\" \"https://api.github.com/repos/$OWNER/$REPO/pulls?state=open\"` |\n| View PR diff | `gh pr diff` | `git diff main...HEAD` (local) or `curl -H \"Accept: application/vnd.github.diff\" ...` |\n| Add comment | `gh pr comment N --body \"...\"` | `curl -X POST .../issues/N/comments -d '{\"body\":\"...\"}'` |\n| Request review | `gh pr edit N --add-reviewer user` | `curl -X POST .../pulls/N/requested_reviewers -d '{\"reviewers\":[\"user\"]}'` |\n| Close PR | `gh pr close N` | `curl -X PATCH .../pulls/N -d '{\"state\":\"closed\"}'` |\n| Check out someone's PR | `gh pr checkout N` | `git fetch origin pull/N/head:pr-N && git checkout pr-N` |\n", "path": "github/github-pr-workflow/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/github/github-pr-workflow", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/ci-troubleshooting.md", "references/conventional-commits.md"], "templates": ["templates/pr-body-bugfix.md", "templates/pr-body-feature.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available", "metadata": {"hermes": {"tags": ["GitHub", "Pull-Requests", "CI/CD", "Git", "Automation", "Merge"], "related_skills": ["github-auth", "github-code-review"]}}}

Never retry with a NEW request id\non timeout (idempotency replays the original job; new id = new power event).\nRate limits: 120 GET/min, 20 POST/min per identity (429 + Retry-After).\nIdempotency persists across restarts (/var/lib/pdu-control/idempotency.json).\n\n## Pitfalls learned\n- secrets.env must stay 640 root:pducontrol (chmod 600 breaks the service).\n- gunicorn must stay --workers 1 (runtime structures are per-process).\n- Dashboard cold-load does 3 serial PDU SSH state reads (~30-60s) — normal.\n- Config JSON at /etc/pdu-control/config.json is the protection source of\n  truth; backup dir /root/pdu-control-backups/20260910-222303/.\n- Rollback: `qm rollback 154 pre_lldap_api_upgrade_20260910` on miam-00133.\n- Deploy via guest-exec needs ≤12KB base64 chunks per write (596 broken pipe\n  on big single posts).\n- LLDAP GraphQL: `user(userId:)` not `user(id:)`; introspect mutations.\n\n## Asset-addressed API (REV4 §10C Phases 2-6, LIVE since 2026-09-27)\n\n- **Asset mapping = Git-managed labels.** Each outlet's label carries `MIAM-##### - <description>`; the FIRST `MIAM-#####` token is the outlet's asset_id (⚠️ labels use 5-digit numbers — a `MIAM-\\d{3}` regex silently matches a 3-digit prefix of `MIAM-00167` and creates phantom duplicates). `build_asset_index()` in `app/api_assets.py` derives the mapping and raises `ASSET_MAPPING_AMBIGUOUS` (500) if one asset_id appears on two outlets.\n- **Raw-route identity guard (Phase 3):** raw outlet actions accept `expected_asset_id` in the JSON body; mismatch → `409 TARGET_IDENTITY_MISMATCH` BEFORE any driver dispatch. Use it to pre-verify identity before a real submit. `GET /capabilities` + `GET /version` expose API version, backend mode, asset count, supported actions, rate limits.\n- **Correlation metadata (Phase 6):** every action accepts `correlation_id` / `source_service` / `upstream_operation_id`; they land in both the human audit line and a structured JSONL audit record (`action_submitted` event). Feed them from ACMS Work / Server Manager ops.\n- **Reusable client (Phase 8):** `pdu_manager_client.py` in the repo root — TLS-verify required by default (verify=False is an explicit opt-out only), NO auto-minted idempotency keys after timeout, structured exceptions (`PduIdentityMismatch`, `PduProtectedPolicy`, `PduAuth`), correlation passthrough, `wait_for_job`. `tests/test_pdu_client.py` covers the contract.\n- **Import-time backend selection:** `app_runtime.py` reads `PDU_BACKEND` at import. Test files MUST set `PDU_BACKEND=mock` in conftest BEFORE importing app modules (a per-test monkeypatch is too late if another test imported app first) — this is the REQ-010 structural \"CI never actuates hardware\" guarantee.\n## Live power-cycle test protocol (learned 2026-09-27, authorized 00119 test)\n\n- **Verified mapping:** `151:9 = MIAM-00119` (PVE node, Dell 7010). OFF dropped the node (~10s), ON restored it, PDU REBOOT (native Cycle Load) cleanly rebooted it. All three legs audit-logged.\n- **⚠️ Power-cut latency is NOT instant:** an outlet can read OFF at the PDU relay for ~25s+ before the attached node actually loses power (capacitors/PSU holdup). A fast ping loop showing \"still up\" right after OFF does NOT prove a wiring mismatch — wait 30s+ minimum before concluding anything.\n- **⚠️ 152:3 = miam-00135 = a LIVE Proxmox node** (hosts VM114 LLM-Manager, VM120, CT122 ACMS). Never dispatch \"wiring probes\" to 152:3. If a power-cycle of it ever happens accidentally: PVE `onboot=1` guests did NOT auto-start (twice) — verify guest states after any node power event, and ACMS containers need a manual `docker compose --env-file /opt/acms/.env -f /opt/acms/repo/deploy/compose.yaml up -d` re-raise. Recovery verified 2026-09-27 (TDR-0002).\n- **Pre-dispatch discipline:** quote the exact `ip + outlet + label` triple from `config/examples/config.example.json` in the dispatch reason; verify target identity against the authoritative config BEFORE the 202, and prefer the least-consequential asset for any probe.\n- Driving actuation via the emergency session: curl with cookie jar (`-c/-b`), `POST /api/action` JSON, then verify via in-process `read_state` (the app's venv python) — job polling from an emergency session 401s on `/api/v1`.\n", "path": "devops/pdu-manager-vm154/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/pdu-manager-vm154", "linked_files": null, "usage_hint": null, "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

{"success": true, "name": "file-write-verification", "description": "Verify every file write/patch against ground truth (git diff, ast.parse, py_compile, bash -n, git checkout to recover) — never trust the tool echo or lint status alone; repair pattern for mid-write content corruption; when to prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites.", "tags": [], "related_skills": [], "content": "---\nname: file-write-verification\ndescription: \"Verify every file write/patch against ground truth (git diff, ast.parse, py_compile, bash -n, git checkout to recover) — never trust the tool echo or lint status alone; repair pattern for mid-write content corruption; when to prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\n---\n\n# File-Write Verification Discipline\n\nClass-level rule for any session that writes or edits files via tool calls.\n\n## Why\n\nIn one session the write channel corrupted content mid-write 5+ times:\nphantom text injected into file contents and into tool-result echoes, writes\ntruncated mid-content, and one malformed call writing to a garbage path\n(`/home/jordatech/work adversely/...`). The tool's own success echo and lint\nstatus both reported \"ok\" on corrupted files. The corruption was invisible\nuntil verified against independent ground truth.\n\n## The rule\n\n**Never trust the write tool's echo or its lint verdict alone.** After every\nwrite/patch of a file that matters:\n\n1. **Verify syntax/structure independently:**\n   - Python: `python3 -m py_compile <f>` or `python3 -c \"import ast; ast.parse(open('<f>').read())\"`\n   - Shell: `bash -n <f>`\n   - YAML/TOML/JSON: parse it\n   - Expected size: sanity-check byte/line count against what you intended\n2. **Verify content against git:** `git diff <f>` is the authoritative record\n   of what actually landed. Review the diff before staging.\n3. **Never run repair loops against echo.** Re-read the file (read_file or\n   git diff) before any fix; assert pre-conditions before surgical edits.\n4. **Prefer patch-tool edits over full rewrites** for existing files — smaller\n   diff surface, independently verified by the fuzzy-match result.\n5. **Use git to recover:** `git checkout -- <f>` restores a known-good state\n   when a write mangles a tracked file; then re-apply changes as small patches.\n\n## Repair pattern (Python, for surgical fixes with pre-checks)\n\n```python\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path(\"path/to/file\")\nlines = p.read_text().splitlines()\n# ASSERT pre-conditions before touching anything — abort on surprise\nassert lines[74] == \"expected exact line\", lines[74]\ndel lines[179:181]  # drop duplicated fragment\np.write_text(\"\\n\".join(lines) + \"\\n\")\n```\n\n## sed -i with quoted replacements silently mangles quoting\n\nMulti-site `sed -i 's|…\\$(dirname \"\\$0\")…|…|'` edits on a deploy script produced\ncorrect-looking but broken quoting on 2 of 3 sites (a closing quote lost inside an\n`if \"$(dirname \"$0\")/healthcheck.sh $([ …` construct), while the third site looked\nright — caught only by printing the affected lines + `bash -n` (2026-09-27). sed's\nown exit code and the shell's silence are not evidence.\n\n- For shell scripts, prefer the patch tool (returns a reviewable unified diff) or a\n  python string-replace with exact-match assertions over `sed -i` when the pattern\n  contains quotes/brackets.\n- If sed is used anyway: immediately `sed -n '<affected>,<affected>p'` + `bash -n` —\n  never proceed on the sed exit code alone.\n\n## Red flags in tool results\n\n- Tool result text containing strings you never wrote (canary/injection\n  phrases, references to files you didn't touch) → treat the ENTIRE result as\n  suspect; verify on disk immediately.\n- A \"sibling subagent modified this file\" warning when you spawned no\n  subagents → usually the tracker reacting to your own unrecorded writes;\n  check git status/diff for ground truth.\n- Write succeeded to a path you didn't specify → `git status --porcelain`\n  for unexpected files, delete them, re-run git-clean checks.\n\n## No-lint file types (extra vigilance)\n\n`.html` (Jinja templates), `.md`, and other gate-less types get\n`lint: skipped` — syntax checks in step 1 are unavailable, so corruption\nsurvives until git diff or a render test. Observed corruption shapes in\nthese types (2026-09-26, two instances): stray closing tags inside `<p>`\n(`…stale guess.</h2></p>`), truncated table cells (`<td><a href=\"…\">{\">`),\nand — via the PATCH tool, not just write_file — a duplicated header line\ninserted mid-file in a markdown log. For these types ALWAYS: (a) review the\n`git diff` hunks line-by-line right after writing, and (b) when a test\nrenders the file, assert one string from every major section so truncation\nfails loudly.\n\n## When verification found corruption (repair order)\n\n1. `git checkout -- <file>` (if tracked and pre-write state is good)\n2. Re-apply intended change via patch tool (small, verifiable diffs)\n3. For untracked new files: python re-write with assertion pre-checks\n4. Re-verify: syntax check + git diff review + (for Python) import test\n\n## Non-durable failures (do NOT capture as rules)\n\nMissing binaries, path mismatches after migration, transient tool outages\nthat resolved in-session — these are environment state, not discipline. # AGENTS.md — LLM Manager

These instructions apply to AI agents modifying the LLM Manager repository or MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager deployment.

## 1. Read before changing anything

Read, in order:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `FUTURE_WORK.md`
4. `SPRINT.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_STATUS.md`
7. `docs/OPERATIONS_AND_USAGE.md`
8. relevant ADRs/TDRs

Approved requirements define scope. Future work is not authorization to implement.

## 2. Service boundary

LLM Manager owns inference/runtime infrastructure concerns. ACMS owns work/project/context orchestration.

Do not move project requirements, Kanban/task state, persistent transcript history, human steering governance, or general ACMS context management into LLM Manager merely because an integration needs them.

## 3. Production safety rules

- Treat MARION-IA-USA as production infrastructure.
- Never replace a known-working model/runtime before capturing its exact rollback configuration.
- Put deployments into explicit maintenance before authorized manual work.
- Recovery has historically had more than one trigger; verify both desired service state and desired power/VM state before stopping a VM.
- Do not assume a stopped model stays stopped until recovery state is verified.
- Never start two VMs that share the same passthrough GPU devices.
- Avoid repeated rapid GPU-VM stop/start cycles on MIAM-00111; a known PCIe/AER/riser condition has previously required a host reboot to recover.
- Preserve custom/patched vLLM environments. Do not `pip install -U vllm` into a known-good runtime.
- Experimental model stacks belong in versioned paths/containers/VMs.

## 4. Configuration and model truth

The live registry and verified runtime state are authoritative for current deployments.

Do not infer that an alias is local merely from its name. Verify provider/runtime/model identity.

When documenting a model deployment, capture:

- actual artifact/revision/checksum;
- runtime/image/commit;
- quantization;
- host/VM/site;
- context;
- TP/PP/DP/EP and relevant runtime flags;
- RAM/VRAM/hardware;
- route/alias;
- health and benchmark evidence.

## 5. History and rollback

Deployment history must make engineering work reproducible.

- `Load Configuration` is non-destructive.
- `Restore & Serve` is a separate confirmed operation.
- Failed experiments may be retained but hidden by default.
- Do not delete the last known-good revision merely because a newer model works.

## 6. Benchmark discipline

A performance number without deployment context is misleading.

Link benchmarks to exact deployment revisions and record at least:

- TTFT;
- output tokens/sec per request/agent;
- aggregate output tokens/sec;
- concurrency;
- prompt/output token counts;
- tested context;
- RAM/VRAM;
- host/hardware/runtime;
- errors;
- representative Hermes/tool-use status when relevant.

When comparing models, prefer same-node tests. When comparing nodes, use the same model/configuration first so hardware effects are visible.

## 7. Secrets and data

Never commit:

- `.env` files containing secrets;
- API keys/tokens;
- passwords;
- SSH/private keys;
- production database row dumps;
- customer-sensitive data;
- raw logs containing credentials.

Sanitize configuration into `.example` files. Run a secret scan before commit. If a secret appears in a tool transcript or repository candidate, stop and surface it for rotation/removal.

## 8. Git workflow

- Work on a dedicated branch.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description`.
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Do not invent an issue ID; create/use an approved issue according to repository policy.
- Keep capture/bootstrapping changes reviewable.
- A human must approve before merge.

## 9. ADR policy

If a human explicitly made a significant architecture decision, record it as an ADR and surface it in the PR.

If the agent thinks a significant new decision is required, draft `Status: Proposed` and stop for human approval before treating it as accepted.

## 10. TDR policy

Use a TDR for intentional shortcuts, patched runtimes, manual recovery procedures, missing automation, known hardware/software limitations, or other accepted debt.

## 11. Validation

Run the exact project validation commands once captured/documented. At minimum for a Python service, consider syntax/import checks, tests, config parsing, migration/schema verification, and API smoke tests in a safe environment.

Report `Pass`, `Fail`, or `Not run` accurately. Never infer a passing test.

## 12. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict;
- an ACMS-facing contract would be invented or materially changed;
- a destructive production action is required outside the approved maintenance scope;
- credentials/access are required and unavailable;
- a security boundary changes;
- production tests fail unexpectedly;
- a new persistent model policy would be made without approved authority;
- the diff expands beyond the approved capture/documentation scope.

## 13. Handoff standard

At completion provide:

1. requirements addressed;
2. source/code captured and source locations;
3. files excluded and why;
4. changes made;
5. validation commands/results;
6. production impact (if any);
7. ADR/TDR status;
8. future-work items discovered;
9. known risks/unresolved questions;
10. exact branch, commit, PR, and recommended next action.

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous coding agents working in this repository.

## 1. Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, and approve merges.

Agents may:

- inspect the repository;
- propose a short plan;
- implement approved requirements;
- create or update automated tests;
- update documentation affected by the change;
- add newly discovered ideas to `FUTURE_WORK.md`;
- draft ADRs and TDRs;
- run non-destructive validation;
- prepare commits and pull requests.

Agents must not:

- silently add product scope;
- promote future work into approved requirements without human approval;
- accept their own newly invented significant architecture decision;
- merge their own work under the current operating model;
- commit directly to `main`/production branches;
- use destructive commands, rotate secrets, alter production data, change permissions, or modify infrastructure without explicit human approval;
- hide failed validation, security concerns, or unresolved trade-offs.

## 2. Read before planning

For every meaningful change, read the relevant parts of:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
5. relevant files in `docs/adr/`
6. relevant files in `docs/tdr/`
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope or follow-up work is involved

Repository-local instructions and approved requirements are the primary source of truth.

## 3. Requirement discipline

Implementation work should trace to one or more `REQ-###` entries.

Before editing, summarize:

- the requirement IDs being implemented;
- the acceptance criteria;
- the files/components likely to change;
- assumptions or ambiguities;
- the validation that will prove the change works.

If the requested work is not covered by an approved requirement, do one of the following:

- ask for human approval to add/modify the requirement; or
- record the idea in `FUTURE_WORK.md` if it is not part of the current scope.

Do not invent scope merely because it seems useful.

## 4. Planning and execution

Before meaningful edits, provide a short implementation plan. Keep the plan proportional to the change.

During execution:

- work in a dedicated branch/worktree/sandbox;
- keep the diff focused;
- follow the existing architecture and project conventions;
- prefer readable, conventional code over clever code;
- avoid unrelated cleanup;
- preserve backward compatibility unless an approved requirement or ADR says otherwise;
- update affected documentation in the same change.

If the implementation grows materially beyond the approved plan, stop and surface the scope increase.

## 5. Significant decisions and ADRs

Create or update an ADR when a change makes a significant, durable decision about architecture, major dependencies, data/storage, external integrations, security boundaries, deployment model, scalability approach, or another choice that future maintainers are likely to ask "why did we choose this?"

### Human-directed decision

If a human explicitly made the decision, the agent may record it as `Status: Accepted`. The ADR must still be called out in the pull request so the human sees what was recorded.

### Agent-originated decision

If the agent concludes a significant decision is needed, create a draft ADR with `Status: Proposed` and surface it for human approval. Do not treat it as approved until a human accepts it.

Use `docs/adr/TEMPLATE.md`.

## 6. Technical debt and TDRs

Create a TDR when a change intentionally leaves known technical debt such as a shortcut, workaround, temporary design, missing test, manual step, or deferred reliability/security/performance/maintainability improvement.

Use `docs/tdr/TEMPLATE.md` and keep the record concise.

An agent may propose a TDR, but the human reviewer should confirm that the debt is acceptable and that the owner is appropriate.

## 7. Validation

Use the exact validation commands documented by the project once the implementation stack is selected.

Run all checks relevant to the change, such as:

- formatting;
- lint/static analysis;
- type checking where applicable;
- unit tests;
- integration tests;
- end-to-end tests where applicable;
- build/package validation;
- security/secret/dependency checks;
- deployment or readiness checks when relevant.

Report exactly what passed, failed, or was not run. Never imply that a check passed if it was skipped or unavailable.

If expected commands are not documented, surface that as an agent-readiness gap instead of guessing silently.

## 8. Security and data handling

- Do not commit secrets, credentials, private keys, access tokens, or sensitive customer/company data.
- Treat generated shell commands, migrations, dependencies, and configuration changes as untrusted until reviewed.
- Do not weaken authentication, authorization, encryption, logging, isolation, or monitoring merely to make a task easier.
- Surface security-sensitive behavior for explicit human review.

## 9. Git and pull requests

Startup Teams Git policy applies unless a stricter project policy is documented.

- Use a dedicated branch; direct commits to `main`/production branches are prohibited.
- Branch format: `type/ticket-short-description` (for example, `feat/AGR-24-bnpl-checkout`).
- Commit format: `type(scope): short description (#issue-id)`.
- Commit scope is required.
- Every commit must reference the related issue/ticket.
- Keep commits and PRs small enough to review.
- Required validation must pass before merge.
- A human reviewer must approve before merge under the current operating model.

Every PR should state:

- feature / requirement IDs;
- what changed and why;
- files/components changed;
- validation performed and results;
- known risks or limitations;
- documentation changes;
- ADRs/TDRs added or changed;
- future-work items discovered;
- reviewer focus areas.

## 10. Stop and ask for human judgment when

- requirements conflict or are materially ambiguous;
- a new significant ADR decision is required;
- credentials or restricted access are required;
- a destructive action is needed;
- tests fail unexpectedly and the cause is not understood;
- security-sensitive behavior changes;
- production data or infrastructure would be changed;
- the diff grows substantially beyond the planned scope;
- a future-work idea would need to become approved product scope.

## 11. Handoff standard

At the end of a work session or before handing work to another agent/human, provide:

1. requirement IDs addressed;
2. concise summary of changes;
3. files changed;
4. validation commands and results;
5. known risks / unresolved questions;
6. ADR/TDR status;
7. future-work items discovered;
8. recommended next action.

## 12. PDU Manager production safety additions

This application can control real electrical power. Treat production interactions as high-consequence infrastructure operations.

Agents MUST NOT perform real PDU ON/OFF/REBOOT/CYCLE operations as tests unless a human explicitly authorizes the exact outlet/action for that session.

During normal development, source capture, CI, staging, or deployment verification:

# AGENTS.md

Instructions for autonomous and semi-autonomous agents working in ACMS.

## Authority model

Humans set product intent, approve scope, approve significant decisions, define mandatory governance boundaries, and approve merges.

Agents may inspect the repository, plan, implement approved requirements, decompose approved scope into tasks, create tests, update affected documentation, create handoffs, add out-of-scope discoveries to `FUTURE_WORK.md`, draft ADRs/TDRs, and execute non-destructive work inside explicitly approved scope/budget/security/environment boundaries.

Agents must not silently add/remove product scope, promote future work, accept their own new significant ADR, merge their own feature work, exceed a budget, cross tenant/context boundaries, deploy to production, alter credentials/permissions, perform destructive infrastructure actions, or modify protected Organization/Product/Project/Feature context without required human authorization.

## Read before planning

For every meaningful change read:

1. `README.md`
2. `REQUIREMENTS.md`
3. `SPRINT.md`
4. `docs/BUSINESS_PROBLEMS.md`
5. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`
6. relevant accepted/proposed ADRs
7. `FUTURE_WORK.md` when scope/follow-up work is involved
8. `INITIAL_IDEAS.md` when source provenance matters

## Requirement discipline

Implementation must trace to one or more `ACMS-REQ-###` entries. Before editing summarize the requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, files/components likely to change, assumptions, and validation.

If work is outside approved requirements, ask for human approval or record it in `FUTURE_WORK.md`.

## Future-work provenance

Every future-work item must identify one of:

- **Human-Directed**
- **Agent-Discovered**
- **Source-Derived**

Human-Directed does not mean approved; it remains future scope until promoted.

## Centralized governance, decentralized execution

ACMS controls approved assignment, scope, budget, context access, permissions, and governance. It must not become the synchronous permission service for every shell command/tool call/test/retry.

A worker agent may autonomously execute implementation details inside its approved envelope.

An agent may have:

- one primary ACMS work assignment; and
- zero or more registered background routines/cron jobs.

## Context authority

Protected context levels:

- Organization
- Product
- Project
- Feature

Agents may propose changes but may not modify these without human authorization or explicit delegated authority.

Agent, Task, and Live Session context may be modified within approved policy.

If approved scope appears wrong, execute it as far as reasonably possible, preserve state, create a handoff, and propose the alternate direction instead of silently changing scope.

## Handoffs

Meaningful work must produce/update a Markdown handoff recording:

1. assigned goal and requirement IDs;
2. what was attempted;
3. what actually changed;
4. what the agent believes the current state is;
5. validation and results;
6. blockers/assumptions/unresolved questions;
7. recommended next action.

## ADR policy

Human-explicit durable architecture decisions may be recorded as `Accepted`. Agent-originated significant decisions remain `Proposed` until human approval.

## Approval timeout policy

- Reversible in-scope decision: checkpoint/preserve state, use documented fallback, continue if safe.
- Consequential but deferable action: defer that action and continue unrelated work.
- Mandatory human gate: never infer approval from timeout; hold/deny the gated action and continue only unrelated authorized work.

## Security

- Never commit credentials/secrets/customer-sensitive data.
- External/customer agents are a separate trust domain.
- Internal Agent Bridge endpoints are private-network/VPN endpoints unless an accepted ADR says otherwise.
- Do not weaken authentication/authorization/audit to simplify implementation.

## Git workflow

The documentation bootstrap is human-authorized for direct import. After bootstrap, feature work must use a dedicated branch and PR.

Recommended branch: `type/ACMS-short-description`.

Recommended commit: `type(scope): short description (ACMS-REQ-###)` plus issue/ticket when available.

Every feature PR must list requirement IDs, acceptance criteria, validation, risks, ADR/TDR changes, future work, and reviewer focus. Agents do not self-merge.

{"output": "# ADR-0011 — Server Manager modular stack + Agent Runtime Manager as peer service\n\nStatus: Accepted (human-approved direction, FINAL_DRAFT_REV4 §1/§5/§7, executed 2026-09-27)\n\n## Context\n\nREV4 approved a Server Manager structure (LLM Manager + Agent Runtime Manager in one\nrepository, split hosting deferred), Python 3.12+/FastAPI/SQLAlchemy 2.x/Alembic as the\napproved stack, and separate logical databases per service (acms / llmmanager /\nagent_runtime_manager) with no cross-database foreign keys.\n\n## Decision\n\n1. New package `server_manager/` with subpackages `common/`, `llm_manager/`, `agent_runtime_manager/`.\n2. Strangler staging: existing proven LLM Manager modules (service/app/*) remain the live\n   implementations; `server_manager/llm_manager/adapters.py` re-exports them lazily so new\n   code imports through `server_manager.*`. Old paths are removed only after parity (§5).\n3. Agent Runtime Manager is GREENFIELD on SQLAlchemy 2.x ORM + Alembic (its own database\n   `agent_runtime_manager`, own credentials file, no cross-DB FKs). ACMS correlation is via\n   UUIDs (acms_agent_id string column), never foreign keys.\n4. Machine API v1 mounted in a NEW uvicorn service (`server-manager-api`, port 8300):\n   - ARM: POST /api/v1/agent-runtimes, GET /api/v1/provisioning-jobs/{job_id},\n     GET /api/v1/agent-runtimes[/{id}], POST desired-state, DELETE (ownership-gated).\n   - LLM: GET /api/v1/model-routes/{route}, GET /api/v1/usage (read-only this sprint).\n5. Auth: scoped bearer service identities from a 0600 tokens file (§11). TDR-0007 records\n   the mTLS debt.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- Two DBs to operate; alembic authority is per-DB (ARM now; llmmanager keeps its own ledger\n  tooling until its staged ORM migration completes — TDR-0008).\n- The provisioning state machine mirrors the proven agentmanager engine but adds durable\n  on-VM ownership markers (§8) that the vertical slice lacked.\n- The existing agentmanager vertical slice (VM121 template path) is superseded by ARM for\n  provisioning; its UI remains until parity.\n\n## Related\n\n- TDR-0007 (service-token → mTLS), TDR-0008 (llmmanager ORM staging), FINAL_DRAFT_REV4.", "exit_code": 0, "error": null}

Documented TDR: temporary\n11|service token → future mTLS (plan §11/§19).\n12|\"\"\"\n13|from __future__ import annotations\n14|\n15|import hashlib\n16|import hmac\n17|import os\n18|from dataclasses import dataclass\n19|from functools import lru_cache\n20|\n21|from fastapi import HTTPException, Request\n22|\n23|# Scope names (least-privilege defaults per §10B/§13)\n24|SCOPES_READ = {\"runtime:read\", \"job:read\", \"route:read\", \"usage:read\", \"health:read\"}\n25|SCOPES_WRITE = {\"runtime:write\", \"runtime:destroy\", \"job:submit\"}\n26|ALL_SCOPES = SCOPES_READ | timezone\n13|\n14|from sqlalchemy import (\n15|    BigInteger,\n16|    Boolean,\n17|    DateTime,\n18|    Enum,\n19|    ForeignKey,\n20|    Index,\n21|    Integer,\n22|    String,\n23|    Text,\n24|    UniqueConstraint,\n25|    func,\n26|)\n27|from sqlalchemy.dialects.postgresql import JSONB, UUID\n28|from sqlalchemy.ext.mutable import MutableDict\n29|from sqlalchemy.orm import DeclarativeBase, Mapped, mapped_column, relationship\n30|\n31|\n32|def utcnow() -> datetime:\n33|    return datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n34|\n35|\n36|class Base(DeclarativeBase):\n37|    pass\n38|\n39|\n40|class RuntimeState(str, enum.Enum):\n41|    DESIRED_RUNNING = \"DESIRED_RUNNING\"\n42|    DESIRED_STOPPED = \"DESIRED_STOPPED\"\n43|    DESIRED_DESTROYED = \"DESIRED_DESTROYED\"\n44|\n45|\n46|class ActualState(str, enum.Enum):\n47|    ABSENT = \"ABSENT\"            # no VM created yet\n48|    PROVISIONING = \"PROVISIONING\"\n49|    RUNNING = \"RUNNING\"\n50|    STOPPED = \"STOPPED\"\n51|    ERROR = \"ERROR\"\n52|    DESTROYED = \"DESTROYED\"\n53|\n54|\n55|class JobState(str, enum.Enum):\n56|    QUEUED = \"QUEUED\"\n57|    RUNNING = \"RUNNING\"\n58|    DONE = \"DONE\"\n59|    FAILED = \"FAILED\"\n60|    CANCELLED = \"CANCELLED\"\n61|\n62|\n63|class AgentRuntime(Base):\n64|    \"\"\"A replaceable runtime identity underneath an ACMS persistent agent (§1).\"\"\"\n65|\n66|    __tablename__ = \"agent_runtimes\"\n67|\n68|    runtime_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(UUID(as_uuid=True), primary_key=True, default=uuid.uuid4)\n69|    acms_agent_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), index=True)  # correlation UUID from ACMS, no FK (§1)\n70|    provisioning_request_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), index=True)  # idempotency key from ACMS\n71|    name: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(80))\n72|\n73|    provider: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(40), default=\"proxmox_vm\")\n74|    node: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(40), nullable=True)\n75|    vmid: Mapped[int | None] = mapped_column(Integer, nullable=True, index=True)\n76|\n77|    harness: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(40), default=\"hermes\")\n78|    runtime_class: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(40), default=\"software_development_worker\")\n79|    site: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(40), default=\"marion-ia-usa\")\n80|    model_route: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(80), nullable=True)\n81|\n82|    desired_state: Mapped[RuntimeState] = mapped_column(Enum(RuntimeState, name=\"arm_runtime_desired_state\"),\n83|                                                        default=RuntimeState.DESIRED_RUNNING)\n84|    actual_state: Mapped[ActualState] = mapped_column(Enum(ActualState, name=\"arm_runtime_actual_state\"),\n85|                                                      default=ActualState.ABSENT)\n86|\n87|    # §8 ownership metadata — REQUIRED before any destructive action.\n88|    # MutableDict: the service mutates this dict in place; without mutation\n89|    # tracking SQLAlchemy never flags the attribute dirty and the mutations\n90|    # are silently dropped on commit.\n91|    created_by_agent_runtime_manager: Mapped[bool] = mapped_column(Boolean, default=False, server_default=\"false\")\n92|    template_source: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(120), nullable=True)\n93|    ownership_meta: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(MutableDict.as_mutable(JSONB), default=dict)\n94|\n95|    # §10A Hermes state backup metadata\n96|    hermes_state_repo: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(120), nullable=True)\n97|    hermes_state_branch: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(120), nullable=True)\n98|    hermes_profile_name: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(120), nullable=True)\n99|    last_state_commit_sha: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(64), nullable=True)\n100|    state_sync_health: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(20), nullable=True)\n101|\n102|    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())\n103|    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now(), onupdate=func.now())\n104|\n105|    jobs: Mapped[list[\"ProvisioningJob\"]] = relationship(back_populates=\"runtime\", lazy=\"selectin\")\n106|\n107|\n108|class ProvisioningJob(Base):\n109|    \"\"\"Async provisioning job with idempotency (§10/§13).\"\"\"\n110|\n111|    __tablename__ = \"provisioning_jobs\"\n112|\n113|    job_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(UUID(as_uuid=True), primary_key=True, default=uuid.uuid4)\n114|    request_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), unique=True, index=True)  # idempotency key\n115|    runtime_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(ForeignKey(\"agent_runtimes.runtime_id\", ondelete=\"CASCADE\"))\n116|    requested_by: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(60))  # service identity\n117|    authority: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(60), default=\"sprint_execution_context\")  # §14\n118|    state: Mapped[JobState] = mapped_column(Enum(JobState, name=\"arm_job_state\"), default=JobState.QUEUED)\n119|    error: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(Text, nullable=True)\n120|\n121|    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())\n122|    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now(), onupdate=func.now())\n123|\n124|    runtime: Mapped[AgentRuntime] = relationship(back_populates=\"jobs\")\n125|    steps: Mapped[list[\"ProvisioningStep\"]] = relationship(back_populates=\"job\", lazy=\"selectin\",\n126|                                                           order_by=\"ProvisioningStep.seq\")\n127|\n128|\n129|class ProvisioningStep(Base):\n130|    __tablename__ = \"provisioning_steps\"\n131|\n132|    id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(BigInteger, primary_key=True, autoincrement=True)\n133|    job_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID] = mapped_column(ForeignKey(\"provisioning_jobs.job_id\", ondelete=\"CASCADE\"))\n134|    seq: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(Integer)\n135|    step: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(60))\n136|    state: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), default=\"RUNNING\")  # RUNNING/DONE/FAILED\n137|    detail: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(Text, nullable=True)\n138|    updated_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())\n139|\n140|    job: Mapped[ProvisioningJob] = relationship(back_populates=\"steps\")\n141|    __table_args__ = (UniqueConstraint(\"job_id\", \"seq\", name=\"uq_provstep_job_seq\"),)\n142|\n143|\n144|class RuntimeEvent(Base):\n145|    \"\"\"Runtime audit (§8: every destructive action records an audit event).\"\"\"\n146|\n147|    __tablename__ = \"runtime_events\"\n148|\n149|    id: Mapped[int] = mapped_column(BigInteger, primary_key=True, autoincrement=True)\n150|    runtime_id: Mapped[uuid.UUID | None] = mapped_column(UUID(as_uuid=True), nullable=True, index=True)\n151|    correlation_id: Mapped[str | None] = mapped_column(String(80), nullable=True, index=True)\n152|    actor: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(60))\n153|    action: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(60))\n154|    result: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(20), default=\"ok\")\n155|    detail: Mapped[dict] = mapped_column(JSONB, default=dict)\n156|    created_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())\n157|\n158|\n159|class BridgeRegistration(Base):\n160|    \"\"\"Agent Bridge registration correlation (§15): runtime ↔ ACMS identity link.\"\"\"\n161|\n162|    __tablename__ = \"bridge_registrations\"\n163|\n164|    id: Mapped[int] = acms_agent_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(64), index=True)\n167|    bridge_credential_id: Mapped[str] = mapped_column(String(80))\n168|    last_heartbeat_at: Mapped[datetime | None] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), nullable=True)\n169|    registered_at: Mapped[datetime] = mapped_column(DateTime(timezone=True), server_default=func.now())\n170|\n171|    __table_args__ = = API_CONTRACT_VERSION\n88|\n89|\n90|class JobOut(BaseModel):\n91|    job_id: str\n92|    request_id: str\n93|    runtime_id: str\n94|    state: str\n95|    error: str | None\n96|    authority: acms_agent_id=r.acms_agent_id,\n103|        provisioning_request_id=r.provisioning_request_id, name=r.name,\n104|        provider=r.provider, node=r.node, vmid=r.vmid, harness=r.harness,\n105| created_by_agent_runtime_manager=r.created_by_agent_runtime_manager,\n108|        hermes_state_repo=r.hermes_state_repo, hermes_state_branch=r.hermes_state_branch,\n109| ----------------------------------------------------------------- endpoints\n115|@router.post(\"/agent-runtimes\", status_code=202)\n116|def create_agent_runtime(body: CreateRuntimeRequest, ident: site=body.site,\n123|            model_route=body.model_route, bridge_profile=body.bridge_profile,\n124|            desired_state=RuntimeState(body.desired_state),\n125|        )\n126|        job, can also be drained by a loop\n129|            svc.run_job(session, job.job_id)\n130|            session.refresh(job)\n131|    return {\"job\": JobOut(\n132|        job_id=str(job.job_id), \"state\": st.state, \"detail\": st.detail} for st in job.steps],\n135|    ), \"created\": created}\n136|\n137|\n138|@router.get(\"/provisioning-jobs/{job_id}\")\n139|def get_provisioning_job(job_id: uuid.UUID(job_id))\n143|        if job is None:\n144|            raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=\"job not found\")\n145|        return JobOut(\n146|            job_id=str(job.job_id),
